Root cause, confirmed directly from the error text: `"Transaction API error: Transaction already closed... The timeout for this transaction was 5000 ms, however 5218 ms passed since the start of the transaction."` — Prisma's default 5-second interactive-transaction timeout, exceeded by round-trip network latency to a remote Neon endpoint. CI's own `integration` job avoids this entirely by running against a local `postgres:16` service container (near-zero latency) rather than a database reached over the internet — the difference here is the environment (a real remote database was the only real-Postgres option available in this session), not a code regression from any merge. Re-running just these 3 files in isolation (not under the full 34-file suite's contention) reproduced the same 2 accounting-concurrency failures with the identical timeout message; `pharmacy-clinical-upgrades.test.ts` passed cleanly on its own (11/11), confirming its earlier failure was contention from running alongside 33 other files, not a hook problem in that test itself. **Not fixed as part of this pass** — raising Prisma's default interactive-transaction timeout (or scoping a longer one to these specific `$transaction` calls in `src/modules/accounting/service.ts`/`src/modules/pos/service.ts`) would need its own deliberate change, reviewed on its own, since it touches shared code other branches may also be editing.
- No live tenant login was performed to click through the new "Revenue by source" UI in a browser; only the unauthenticated-redirect behavior and the absence of new runtime errors were confirmed on the live deployment.
## 2026-08-21: Work & Pay assigned-driver client correction

New Work & Pay contracts no longer accept a free-text client name. The selected vehicle must have an active assigned Fleet driver, and the service derives both `driverId` and the historical `clientName` snapshot from that assignment inside the tenant-scoped contract creation transaction. The vehicle selector lists only eligible vehicles and shows each assigned driver's name. A clear message instructs managers to assign a driver first when none is eligible.

Driver remittance authorization now requires the active Work & Pay contract to match both the assigned vehicle and the authenticated driver's stored contract link. This prevents a newly assigned vehicle driver from seeing or paying an earlier driver's contract. Existing contracts are backfilled from each vehicle's current assigned driver when migration `20260821213000_fleet_work_pay_driver_link` runs; records that cannot be linked safely retain their existing name snapshot with a null driver relationship.

Important files: `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260821213000_fleet_work_pay_driver_link/migration.sql`, `src/modules/fleet/service.ts`, Fleet Work & Pay page and actions, and Fleet unit and integration tests. Prisma format, validation, generation, TypeScript, ESLint, and `git diff --check` passed. Focused validation passed 3 files and 26 tests. Migration `20260821213000_fleet_work_pay_driver_link` applied successfully to the isolated Neon branch `test/codex-fleet-work-pay-driver-link`, and both directly affected real-PostgreSQL Fleet files passed 16 tests. ## 2026-08-21: Fleet payment review control and Driver navigation correction

The production payment review defect was traced to the shared Base UI button primitive. Unlike a native HTML button, that primitive defaults to `type="button"`. The Driver-recorded payment Approve and Reject controls did not declare `type="submit"`, so clicking either control never submitted its Server Action. Vercel recorded the surrounding page requests as HTTP 200 and no exception was raised, while a read-only production database check confirmed the photographed Affordit Ventures remittance remained `PENDING` with no reviewer or Fleet payment.

Review controls now submit an explicit hidden `approve` or `reject` decision, declare `type="submit"`, disable during processing, display progress, and return a clear success or error message. The action validates both the tenant-scoped submission ID and decision, logs structured failures, and distinguishes a submission that another manager has already reviewed. The tenant audit event is attempted inside the existing transaction and commits with the review when written. Approved submissions still create the verified Fleet ledger payment and update Work & Pay balances atomically where applicable.

The exact system Driver role no longer sees the organization module catalogue, cross-module Reports link, or header module launcher. Direct requests to `/app/modules` and `/app/reports` return that role to its assignment-scoped dashboard. The driver retains Overview, Notifications, the Fleet Driver Workspace, assigned-vehicle maintenance, targets, contracts, and personal remittance history. Custom roles are not restricted by a matching display name alone, so administrators can deliberately grant broader multi-module access through a custom role.

Important files: `src/app/app/fleet/payments/submission-review-controls.tsx`, Fleet payment page and actions, `src/modules/fleet/service.ts`, `src/lib/auth/permissions.ts`, workspace navigation, Overview and Fleet layouts, workspace Modules and Reports pages, and `test/fleet-driver-sales-controls.test.ts`. No schema or environment change is required. Focused validation passed 2 files and 10 tests. TypeScript and ESLint passed. The full mocked suite passed 82 files and 485 tests, including the editorial punctuation rule. The Next.js 16.2.12 production build compiled successfully and generated all 208 static pages. `git diff --check` passed.

Release commit `60dd4d581739973a3c21c0a283ee6af0da03699a` was pushed to the remote feature branch and fast-forwarded to `main` after Vercel preview deployment `dpl_9X82NHw4Wt7UK3Vj7nzrjceFmMsf` reached `READY`. Production deployment `dpl_99wgTdi1UBUjGsD1yA4GVGTibej9` then reached `READY` on `app.rockfrostgroup.com`, `admin.rockfrostgroup.com`, `www.rockfrostgroup.com`, and `rockfrostgroup.com`. Post-deploy health returned HTTP 200 with `database: reachable`; anonymous requests to Fleet payments, workspace Modules, and workspace Reports returned HTTP 307 to `/login`; and the production HTTP 500 query returned no records. The error clusters contained only the three expected missing-membership records created by those deliberate anonymous probes. No migration was required. A fresh real-PostgreSQL integration run was not observed locally because no disposable PostgreSQL runtime or `TEST_DATABASE_URL` was available; the change preserves the already-covered Fleet approval transaction and does not alter schema or financial calculations.

## 2026-08-21: Fleet remittance workflow and HR membership synchronization

Fleet driver self-service now reflects the actual business flow. A driver first pays the company outside the application, then records that completed payment against the assigned vehicle. The form uses explicit daily vehicle remittance, weekly vehicle remittance, and Work & Pay instalment obligations. It captures payment date, cash, mobile money, bank transfer, card, cheque, or other method, and requires a transaction reference for non-cash payments. Managers verify receipt before a driver record becomes a verified Fleet ledger entry. Drivers remain restricted to assigned vehicles and their own records.

Work & Pay contracts now support either daily or weekly instalments through `paymentSchedule`, `scheduledPaymentAmount`, and `remainingPaymentPeriods`. The legacy `weeklyPaymentAmount` and `remainingDurationWeeks` columns remain populated for backward compatibility. Approval of a driver-recorded Work & Pay payment still performs the contract balance, completion percentage, status, and ledger update atomically. Authorized office staff can record a payment received directly by the office with its date, method, reference, and audit event.

Administration now integrates active internal memberships with Human Resources & Payroll when HR is enabled. Invitation acceptance, active role changes, member reactivation, HR module activation, and the HR employee-list compatibility backfill call one tenant-scoped synchronization service. It creates only a missing linked `HrEmployee`, preserves every existing HR-managed field, uses a PostgreSQL advisory lock for idempotency, writes status history and a tenant audit event, and excludes the external `Vehicle Owner` and `Investor` roles.

Schema and migration: `prisma/migrations/20260821200000_fleet_remittance_schedule` adds the Work & Pay schedule, instalment amount, and remaining-payment-period fields; backfills existing weekly contract data; and adds positive-value checks. Important files include `src/modules/fleet/service.ts`, `src/modules/hr/service.ts`, Fleet driver/payments/Work & Pay pages and actions, Administration role management, invitation acceptance, and platform module activation.

Local release-candidate validation: Prisma format, validation, and generation passed. TypeScript passed. ESLint passed. The focused suite passed 4 files and 27 tests. The full mocked suite passed 82 files and 483 tests. The Next.js 16.2.12 production build compiled successfully and generated all 208 static pages. `git diff --check` passed, and no newly added line contains the prohibited em dash character. The two new real-PostgreSQL suites cover Fleet payment evidence, daily Work & Pay periods, approval effects, HR enablement, external-role exclusions, tenant scoping, and concurrent idempotency.

Release commit `7bd70ea` was pushed through pull request #6. GitHub security, validation, disposable-PostgreSQL integration, and Vercel preview checks all passed. The pull request merged to `main` as production commit `50290ade14b2338e5bd4e9d4073864e19589cb1f`. Vercel production deployment `dpl_6wyiLu5xpBj8FsU5uqnZtBXMrnsv` reached `READY` on `app.rockfrostgroup.com`, `admin.rockfrostgroup.com`, `www.rockfrostgroup.com`, and `rockfrostgroup.com`. The production build applied migration `20260821200000_fleet_remittance_schedule`, completed the idempotent seed, compiled all 208 static pages, and completed without a build error. Post-deploy health returned HTTP 200 with `database: reachable`; anonymous Driver workspace, Fleet payment review, and HR employee routes returned HTTP 307 to `/login`; the public website returned HTTP 200; and the post-deploy HTTP 500 query returned no records. The error-level log query contained only the expected missing-membership records generated by the three deliberate anonymous route probes, each paired with the correct HTTP 307 authentication redirect.

## 2026-08-21: Fleet driver sales, privacy, Vehicle Owner, and maintenance upgrade

Fleet Management now supports two distinct commercial arrangements without forcing every assigned vehicle into Work & Pay. A vehicle can carry a daily or weekly sales target, or an active Work & Pay contract. A linked driver can submit only the collection types and vehicles assigned to that driver. Ordinary daily and weekly sales are recorded against the vehicle ledger; Work & Pay submissions are recorded against the contract and, after manager approval, atomically update the contract amount paid, outstanding balance, completion percentage, and completion status. Duplicate period submissions are blocked both in the service and by a partial database uniqueness rule.

Driver privacy was tightened. The Driver system role no longer receives the organization-wide `fleet.view` permission. Driver dashboards, Fleet overview redirection, maintenance lists, collection forms, dashboard widgets, and private maintenance photos are scoped to the authenticated driver's linked FleetDriver and assigned vehicles. The driver sees their own targets, contract balance, collection history, and maintenance requests rather than organization totals or other drivers. Managers retain organization-wide operational and financial views.

The new `Vehicle Owner` system role receives only dashboard, owner/investor read access, and AI access. Assigning or accepting this role creates the matching FleetOwner profile idempotently, as the existing Driver role already does for FleetDriver. Vehicle-owner pages fail closed when the user has no linked owner record. Owner portfolios now show linked vehicles and verified revenue. Vehicle ownership changes append to `FleetVehicleOwnershipHistory` instead of silently replacing the previous owner.

Maintenance requests now accept an optional JPEG, PNG, or WebP image up to 1 MB. Images are stored as private FileAsset data and streamed only through the authenticated tenant-scoped route `/api/fleet/maintenance/[requestId]/photo`. Managers may inspect all Fleet maintenance evidence; drivers and vehicle owners are limited to assigned or owned vehicles. The existing approval, owner approval, mechanic assignment, work progress, cost recording, manager verification, timestamped events, and audit behavior remain in place.

The Fleet management dashboard and report now include total vehicles, active drivers, vehicle owners, maintenance vehicles, pending maintenance requests, expiring documents, weekly and monthly verified revenue, Work & Pay outstanding balance, pending driver collections, and recent payments. Payment review now shows vehicle, collection type, covered period, expected target, submitted amount, and variance. Investor reporting includes verified ordinary sales as well as Work & Pay receipts.

Schema and migration: `prisma/migrations/20260821180000_fleet_driver_sales_and_owner_access` adds sales-target and submission-type enums, per-vehicle target fields, submission period and expected-amount snapshots, direct vehicle and contract relations, ownership history, duplicate-submission protection, and removal of the stale global Driver `fleet.view` grant. The migration is additive except for that intentional least-privilege role-grant correction.

Release-candidate validation: Prisma format, validation, and generation passed. TypeScript and ESLint passed. The mocked suite passed 81 files and 481 tests. A fresh disposable PostgreSQL 16 database accepted all 49 migrations, and the full guarded integration suite passed 32 files and 176 tests, including the new Fleet actor-scoping, duplicate-period, concurrent-submission, concurrent-approval, and Work & Pay balance assertions. The Next.js 16.2.12 production build compiled successfully and generated all 208 static pages. The first restricted build attempt could not reach Google Fonts; the same build passed with normal network access. The first GitHub validate run compiled the application and passed every preceding check, then its Next.js TypeScript build worker exceeded Node's default 2 GB heap. The workflow's build step now assigns a 4 GB heap, within the hosted runner's capacity, matching the memory required by this 208-page application. `git diff --check` passed and no new customer-facing em dash character was found. Pull request #5 passed GitHub security, validate, real-PostgreSQL integration, and Vercel preview checks, then merged as production commit `0a80392`. Vercel production deployment `dpl_9KPpEonYeWWAKTNJV8xfCnusmQVC` reached Ready on every Rock Frost production alias. The production build completed the migration and idempotent seed before serving traffic. Post-deploy health returned HTTP 200 with `database: reachable`; anonymous Fleet, Driver workspace, and Maintenance requests redirected to `/login`; and the private maintenance-photo route returned HTTP 401. The error-log scan contained only the three expected missing-membership records created by those deliberate anonymous probes, with no unrelated production error.

## 2026-08-21: Production operations upgrade release

This entry supersedes the branch-only status notes below for Accounting, Inventory, Procurement, Hospital, Pharmacy, and Point of Sale. The combined implementation commit `ef002e6` was pushed to `main` and deployed to Vercel production as `dpl_FsMGJ8n7nrQeuUiMEmTKsSuGD2SF`. The deployment reached `READY` on the production aliases.

- Accounting now has posting periods, source-posting idempotency, reversals, and a shared organization transaction lock that serializes period closing and journal posting.
- Inventory now has governed stock counts and barcode workflows.
- Procurement now has receipts and supplier invoices. Invoice inputs are aggregated by order line and invoice creation is serialized per organization and purchase order, preventing duplicate-line and concurrent over-invoicing.
- Hospital and Pharmacy now have clinical maker-checker controls, result rejection and audit history, duplicate-patient guidance, controlled-drug approval, stock reconciliation, and tenant-safe barcode lookup. Legacy hospital custom-role grants are migrated to the least-privilege entry permissions.
- Point of Sale now has a dynamic 1 to 100 line barcode cart, immutable transaction snapshots, exact split payments, suspended sales, concurrency-safe partial returns into Inventory, and controlled till-variance approval.
- Published monthly prices are now GHS 799 for Inventory, GHS 699 for Accounting, GHS 599 for Point of Sale, GHS 999 for Pharmacy, and GHS 2,499 for Hospital. Bundle pricing was updated in the central pricing registry. Existing stored subscriptions retain their agreed price until renewal or an explicit plan change.

Database migrations included in this release:

- `20260820231500_accounting_posting_foundation`
- `20260820231500_hospital_pharmacy_clinical_upgrades`
- `20260821003000_inventory_counts_and_barcodes`
- `20260821013000_pos_operational_controls`
- `20260821020000_procurement_receipts_invoices`
- `20260821160000_pharmacy_barcode_uniqueness`

## 2026-08-21: Hospital and Pharmacy clinical upgrades (branch `agent/claude-clinical-upgrades`, NOT merged, NOT deployed)

Built in an isolated worktree/branch per an explicit multi-agent orchestration directive: exclusive scope was the first production-ready clinical upgrade tranche for Hospital and Pharmacy, with Codex owning integration and production release. This entry documents what was built and validated on the branch; **no merge or deploy was performed or authorized by this work**.

- **Hospital — duplicate-patient advisory**: `findHospitalPatientDuplicates()` (pre-existing, tested, previously not wired to any UI) is now called from a new client component, `src/app/app/hospital/patients/patient-registration-dialog.tsx`, via a `useActionState`-driven "Check for existing patients" button (`checkPatientDuplicatesAction` in `src/app/app/hospital/actions.ts`). It only returns state — never redirects — so a check never blocks or delays the real submit, which still goes straight to the unmodified `createPatientAction`.
- **Hospital — lab/imaging permission split**: `hospital.lab.manage`/`hospital.imaging.manage` replaced with `hospital.lab.enter`/`hospital.lab.verify`/`hospital.imaging.enter`/`hospital.imaging.verify` in `src/lib/auth/permissions.ts` and the `prisma/seed-data.ts` duplicate (kept identical per that file's own documented constraint). Doctor now holds only `.enter` (ordering); Laboratory Scientist and Radiology Staff hold both halves (today's clinics run this as one role) — maker-checker enforcement, not the permission split, is what actually stops self-verification for those roles.
- **Hospital — maker-checker + rejection**: new `HospitalSettings.labImagingMakerCheckerEnforced` (default `true`, editable on the Settings page) is read inside `verifyHospitalLabResult`/`verifyHospitalImagingFinding`; when enforced and the verifier id equals the enterer id, it throws `HospitalStateError` before any write. New `rejectHospitalLabResult`/`rejectHospitalImagingFinding` let a verifier decline an unverified result/finding (mandatory reason, new `rejectedById`/`rejectedAt`/`rejectionReason` columns) — this reopens the order item for a fresh entry and never mutates or deletes the rejected row. Both verify and reject functions were changed from a plain `update` to a conditional `updateMany` (`where: { verifiedAt: null, rejectedAt: null }` + a `count` check) inside the transaction — the original code had a real, pre-existing lost-update race (two concurrent verifies could both "succeed," the second silently overwriting the first's `verifiedById`), closed as part of this change since maker-checker's entire purpose depends on that guarantee. `enterHospitalImagingFinding` also gained a parity fix: it previously had no guard at all against re-entry over an already-verified finding (lab already had this), so it could silently insert an orphaned duplicate finding.
- **Hospital — audit events**: `logAuditEvent` (previously unused in `src/modules/hospital/service.ts`) is now called inside the same transaction as each mutation for `lab_result.entered/verified/rejected/corrected` and `imaging_finding.entered/verified/rejected/corrected`.
- **Pharmacy — barcode**: `PharmacyBatch` gained a `barcode` field (medicine already had one). New tenant-scoped `findMedicineByBarcode`/`findBatchByBarcode` service functions and a `lookupBarcodeAction` + `BarcodeLookup` client component on the Medicines page. A duplicate active barcode within the same org is rejected at `createMedicine` time (soft application-level check, not a DB unique constraint — deliberate, since the pre-existing `barcode` column had no prior uniqueness guarantee and a hard constraint risks failing to apply against unknown existing production data).
- **Pharmacy — controlled-drug maker-checker**: new `PharmacySettings.controlledDispenseMakerCheckerEnabled` (default `true`). `dispense()` now detects a CONTROLLED-class line and, when enforcement is on, creates the `PharmacyDispensing` row as `PENDING_APPROVAL` with the requested lines stored as JSON (`pendingLines`) instead of allocating a batch or moving any stock. New `approveControlledDispense()`/`rejectControlledDispense()` do the actual FEFO allocation, stock decrement, and `PharmacyRestrictedRegister` entry only on approval; the requester (`dispensedById`) cannot approve or reject their own request (`PharmacyWorkflowError`). New `PharmacyDispensingStatus` values `PENDING_APPROVAL`/`REJECTED`. A pending-approvals card was added to the Dispensing page, gated on a new `pharmacy.restricted.approve` permission (Pharmacy Manager and Pharmacist hold it; Pharmacy Technician does not, matching its existing narrower permission set).
- **Pharmacy — append-only stock reconciliation**: new `PharmacyStockMovement` model/enum backing five new functions (`recordStockCount`, `recordStockAdjustment`, `recordWriteOff`, `recordSupplierReturn`, `recordPatientReturn`), each gated by the existing `pharmacy.stock.manage` permission and wired into a "Reconcile stock" panel per batch on the Stock page. Write-offs/adjustments/supplier returns are blocked from taking a batch negative unless `PharmacySettings.allowNegativeStock` is on (a pre-existing schema field that had no functional effect anywhere until now, and no settings-page control until now — both fixed as part of this work since the new workflows are exactly what that field is for). Patient returns are deliberately record-only (`quantityDelta: 0`) — a returned medication is never silently restocked as dispensable; a pharmacist who inspects and approves it uses a separate stock adjustment. All four quantity-mutating functions run inside the same Postgres advisory lock namespace (`pharmacy-dispense:<organizationId>`) `dispense()`/`reverseDispensing()` already use, closing a lost-update race that existed in the first draft of these functions (a plain read-then-`update` inside the transaction, no lock) before it ever shipped.
- **Integration boundary preserved as required**: no Hospital table or service function reads/writes any Pharmacy table anywhere in this branch (verified by review and by the existing `docs/MODULE_BOUNDARIES.md` contract). `pricing.ts` was not touched. `docs/HOSPITAL_MODULE.md`, `docs/MODULE_BOUNDARIES.md`, and `docs/PHARMACY_AND_HOSPITAL_ROADMAP.md` now document that the future medication-order contract must be idempotent (a client-generated request id so a retried cross-module call applies once) — this is a documented requirement only; no request-id field or actual connection was built.
- **Migration**: exactly one new hand-written migration, `prisma/migrations/20260820231500_hospital_pharmacy_clinical_upgrades/migration.sql` — purely additive (new nullable/defaulted columns, two new enum values on `PharmacyDispensingStatus`, one new enum `PharmacyStockMovementType`, one new table `PharmacyStockMovement`). No destructive changes, no data migration needed, no other model's existing columns altered.
- **Important files**: `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260820231500_hospital_pharmacy_clinical_upgrades/migration.sql` (new), `prisma/seed-data.ts`, `src/lib/auth/permissions.ts`, `src/modules/hospital/service.ts`, `src/modules/pharmacy/service.ts`, `src/app/app/hospital/actions.ts`, `src/app/app/hospital/{laboratory,imaging,patients,settings}/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/hospital/patients/patient-registration-dialog.tsx` (new), `src/app/app/pharmacy/actions.ts`, `src/app/app/pharmacy/{dispensing,stock,medicines,settings}/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/pharmacy/medicines/barcode-lookup.tsx` (new), `docs/HOSPITAL_MODULE.md`, `docs/MODULE_BOUNDARIES.md`, `docs/PHARMACY_AND_HOSPITAL_ROADMAP.md`, `README.md`.
- **Tests**: two new structural/mocked-DB suites (`test/hospital-clinical-upgrades.test.ts`, 11 tests; `test/pharmacy-clinical-upgrades.test.ts`, 14 tests) checking permission wiring, schema shape, and the exact guard strings in the service layer; `test/hospital-module-access.test.ts` updated for the renamed permission key. Two new real-Postgres suites written for the required tenant-isolation/IDOR/concurrency/maker-checker/negative-stock/audit coverage — `test/integration/concurrency/hospital-clinical-upgrades.test.ts` and `test/integration/tenant-isolation/pharmacy-clinical-upgrades.test.ts` — **written but not executed** (see Validation below). Permission-gating (who may call approve/reject/reconcile) is enforced at the Server Action layer, tested by the mocked suites; the integration suites intentionally don't duplicate that RBAC check at the service layer, matching this repo's existing convention.
- **Validation results (exact, as run in this worktree)**:
  - `npx prisma format` — passed, schema formatted cleanly.
  - `npx prisma validate` — passed, "schema is valid."
  - `npx prisma generate` — passed, client generated.
  - `npx tsc --noEmit` — passed, zero errors (run four times across the session, clean every time including after the final edits).
  - `npm run lint` — passed, zero errors, zero warnings (one real violation found and fixed along the way: an unescaped apostrophe in new customer-facing JSX text).
  - `npm run test` (full mocked suite) — **passed: 84 files, 579 tests, zero failures**, including the pre-existing `test/editorial-punctuation.test.ts` em-dash scanner (scoped to `src/app/(public)`, marketing components, and the desktop app — not the authenticated app tree, so it does not by itself cover the Hospital/Pharmacy pages touched here; those were checked manually, see below).
  - `npm run build` — passed, production build compiled successfully, all routes including the changed Hospital/Pharmacy pages generated with no errors (run twice, once mid-session and once after the final edits).
  - `npm run test:integration` — **NOT run.** `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is present as a key in this worktree's copied `.env` but its value is empty; `test/integration/setup/guard.ts` requires a real, reachable, disposable test database and refuses to run without one. The two new integration test files above were written to the same conventions as this repo's existing integration suites (real Postgres, `createTestOrg`/`cleanupTestOrg` fixtures, `Promise.allSettled` for races) and pass `tsc`/lint as part of the whole-project checks, but their actual behavior against a live database has not been observed. This must run and pass before merge — see docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md.
  - Em-dash scan: manual, targeted grep across every file this branch added or changed (not just the narrower pre-existing automated test's scope) — found and fixed 7 occurrences in new customer-facing UI copy or database-stored/rendered text (`src/app/app/pharmacy/dispensing/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/hospital/imaging/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/hospital/patients/patient-registration-dialog.tsx`, and four new `HospitalStateError` messages in `src/modules/hospital/service.ts`, plus one in `src/modules/pharmacy/service.ts`'s patient-return reason string). Pre-existing em dashes in files this branch did not otherwise touch, and in pre-existing error-message strings this branch did not add, were deliberately left alone as out of scope.
  - `git diff --check` — passed, no whitespace errors (only benign CRLF-normalization notices from Git, no actual trailing-whitespace or conflict-marker content).
- **Anything not genuinely verified**: the real-Postgres integration suite (tenant isolation, IDOR, concurrency, maker-checker, negative-stock, audit-row assertions) was written but never executed — no reachable test database in this environment. No browser/E2E verification was performed (no tenant login credentials available in this session, consistent with the same gap noted in the original Hospital module's own handoff entry). CI's `integration` GitHub Actions job (real disposable Postgres) has not run against this branch yet since it has not been pushed as a PR.
- **Expected conflicts against the Accounting and Inventory branches**: none anticipated at the file level — this branch only touches `prisma/schema.prisma` (additively, in Hospital/Pharmacy-only model blocks plus one new back-relation line on `Organization`), `prisma/seed-data.ts` (Hospital/Pharmacy permission constants and role arrays only), `src/lib/auth/permissions.ts` (same), and files exclusively under `src/app/app/hospital/`, `src/app/app/pharmacy/`, `src/modules/hospital/`, `src/modules/pharmacy/`, plus doc files. The only realistic conflict surface is `prisma/schema.prisma`'s `Organization` model (many branches likely add their own back-relation line there) and, if another branch also touches it, `prisma/seed-data.ts`'s permission/role blocks — both are append-style edits that should merge cleanly with a standard three-way merge, but the integrator should re-run `npx prisma format`/`validate`/`generate` after combining branches regardless.

## 2026-08-20: New module — Hostel Management (16th module)

- The user asked for "a 100% full hostel management system," scoped down to a solid foundation plus fee billing after two explicit choices: build the foundation (buildings/rooms/beds, allocations, wardens) with fee billing included in this pass rather than deferred, and use the suggested pricing. Explicitly out of scope for this pass, to check in on next (same staged-build discipline used for every other vertical module): curfew/attendance, visitor logs, incident/disciplinary records, maintenance requests.
- **Design**: a separately subscribable module, a companion to School Management rather than part of it (not every school has a hostel). Occupants are existing `SchoolStudent` records — Hostel owns no student identity, campus, or academic-calendar model of its own, borrowing `SchoolCampus`/`SchoolAcademicYear`/`SchoolTerm` instead. New models: `HostelBuilding` → `HostelRoom` → `HostelBed` (beds A/B/C... generated automatically from a room's capacity), `HostelAllocation`, `HostelWarden`, and `HostelFeeStructure`/`HostelFeeInvoice`/`HostelFeePayment` (a field-for-field mirror of School's own fee models).
- **Concurrency**: `createHostelAllocation` claims a bed with a guarded `AVAILABLE -> OCCUPIED` `updateMany` (same pattern as `markInvoiceSent`), and separately blocks a student who already holds an active allocation elsewhere; `endHostelAllocation` claims the reverse transition the same way before freeing the bed. Fee billing (`issueHostelFeeStructure`, `recordHostelFeePayment`) directly mirrors School's advisory-locked bulk-issue loop and `Decimal`-balance-checked payment guard.
- **Surface**: 7 pages (Overview, Buildings & Rooms, Allocations, Wardens, Fees & Payments, Reports, Settings) plus a dashboard widget and PDF/Excel report export (wired into the shared exporter built earlier this session). Settings is an honest placeholder — no hostel-wide config exists yet. 7 permission keys, 2 new roles ("Hostel Manager", "Warden").
- **Pricing** (user-confirmed): standalone GHS 449/month (GHS 4,490/year, 8 seats, GHS 25/extra seat); new "School & Hostel Complete" bundle (School + Hostel + Accounting + HR) at GHS 1,699/month, alongside the existing "School Complete" bundle rather than replacing it.

## 2026-08-20: Fix — Driver role assignment now creates a matching FleetDriver, plus deploy verification

- The user asked why someone assigned the Driver role from Administration didn't show up on `/app/fleet/drivers`. Root cause: assigning that role only ever changed the member's login permissions (`fleet.driver.self_service`); it never touched the separate `FleetDriver` table the Drivers page lists and driver self-service pages resolve "me" against (`FleetDriver.userId`). A Fleet manager had to remember a second, disconnected manual step. Fix, confirmed with the user before implementing: `ensureFleetDriverForUser()` in `src/modules/fleet/service.ts`, called from `changeMemberRole` and both invitation-acceptance paths (`acceptInvitationNewUser`/`acceptInvitationExistingUser` in `src/lib/auth/invitations.ts`); plus a lazy backfill (`backfillMissingFleetDrivers`, on every `listFleetDrivers` read) so members who got the role before this fix shipped self-heal with no manual migration.

- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build` all clean. Full mocked suite green. CI's `integration` job caught a real bug in the *test*, not the fix: the negative case used the seeded "Organization Owner" role, which holds `ALL_PERMISSIONS` (`prisma/seed-data.ts`) and legitimately includes `fleet.driver.self_service` — the fix correctly created a `FleetDriver` for it, and the test's assumption that it shouldn't was wrong. Fixed to use "Mechanic" (Fleet-related, no driver permission) as the real negative case; CI passed clean on the second run.

## 2026-08-20: Accounting petty cash + Statement of Financial Position, School student/guardian photos

- The user asked to "complete the accounting module" with a petty cash workflow and a Statement of Financial Position, plus photo upload for School students and their guardians. All three build on established patterns rather than new primitives: petty cash and the balance sheet both build on the existing double-entry ledger (`postJournalEntry`/`computeBalance` in `src/modules/accounting/service.ts`); photo upload reuses the exact data-URL storage + authenticated-streaming-route pattern already proven by `InventoryItem.imageData`.
- **Petty cash (imprest system)**: new `AccountingPettyCashFund`/`AccountingPettyCashTransaction` models plus `AccountingPettyCashFundStatus`/`AccountingPettyCashTransactionType` enums (additive migration `20260820120000_add_petty_cash_and_school_photos`). Each fund gets its own dedicated ASSET/`liquidityType: CASH` account, so its balance is always the same journal-line-derived figure every other account uses. `createPettyCashFund` posts the initial float as Debit fund account / Credit Cash (1000). `recordPettyCashExpense` posts Debit [expense category's account or General Expenses] / Credit the fund account, re-checking the fund's row-locked (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) ledger balance and status inside the transaction before posting — the same concurrency pattern `recordInvoicePayment` uses for its overpayment guard, applied here so two concurrent expenses can't together overdraw a fund with room for only one. `replenishPettyCashFund` auto-computes the shortfall back to the float (or accepts an explicit amount). `closePettyCashFund` claims the fund atomically (guarded `updateMany`, same pattern as `markInvoiceSent`) and reverses any remaining float back to Cash. New page `/app/accounting/petty-cash`, gated on the existing `accounting.cashbook.manage` permission (no new permission key — this is cash-account administration, the same class of action cashbook opening balances and reconciliations already require).
- **Statement of financial position**: `getStatementOfFinancialPosition()` groups `listAccounts()`'s balances by ASSET/LIABILITY/EQUITY. Revenue/Expense accounts have no balance-sheet home of their own, so the current period's net income is folded into equity as "Retained earnings (current period)," the live equivalent of a period-end close entry. Rendered as a new card on the existing Reports page (`/app/accounting/reports`), which also reports whether Assets = Liabilities + Equity holds — the check exists to surface a ledger-corruption bug, not because one is expected.
- **School student/guardian photos**: `SchoolStudent.photoData`/`SchoolGuardian.photoData` (nullable `Text`, same migration). Upload validated in `src/lib/school-photo-image.ts` (JPEG/PNG/WebP, magic-byte signature check, 1 MB cap — the inventory item image helper's logic, duplicated rather than shared to avoid coupling two unrelated modules' image-handling to one file). Served through `/api/school/students/[studentId]/photo` and `/api/school/guardians/[guardianId]/photo`, both authenticated and gated on the School module permission. The Students & Guardians page (`/app/school/students`) gained a photo column on both tables; for users with `school.students.manage` it's a small dialog to add/replace/remove the photo. List queries stay lean by fetching only which ids have a photo (`listSchoolStudentPhotoIds`/`listSchoolGuardianPhotoIds`) rather than every row's image bytes.
- Important files: `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260820120000_add_petty_cash_and_school_photos/migration.sql` (new), `src/modules/accounting/service.ts`, `src/app/app/accounting/petty-cash/page.tsx` (new), `src/app/app/accounting/petty-cash/actions.ts` (new), `src/app/app/accounting/reports/page.tsx`, `src/modules/accounting/navigation.tsx`, `src/lib/school-photo-image.ts` (new), `src/app/api/school/students/[studentId]/photo/route.ts` (new), `src/app/api/school/guardians/[guardianId]/photo/route.ts` (new), `src/modules/school/service.ts`, `src/app/app/school/actions.ts`, `src/app/app/school/students/page.tsx`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md`.
- Validation: `npx prisma validate` and `npx prisma generate` passed; `npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors; `npm run lint` passed with zero warnings on every new/changed file; `npm run build` compiled all routes successfully, including the three new ones (`/app/accounting/petty-cash`, `/api/school/students/[studentId]/photo`, `/api/school/guardians/[guardianId]/photo`). New unit tests: `test/petty-cash.test.ts` (validation-before-db-call, the row-locked balance/status re-check, the replenishment shortfall calculation, the atomic close claim — all mocked-Prisma), `test/statement-of-financial-position.test.ts` (the accounting identity holds for a known set of balances, and is correctly flagged false for a deliberately unbalanced one), `test/school-photo-image.test.ts` (mirrors `test/inventory-item-image.test.ts`). Updated `test/module-access.test.ts`'s hardcoded guarded-page/guarded-action counts (94→95, 51→52) to include the new petty-cash page and actions file. Full mocked suite: 78 files, 522 tests, all passing. Checked every new/changed file for em dashes in customer-facing copy per `AGENTS.md`'s punctuation rule; none introduced.

## 2026-08-19: Publish Terms of Service and Privacy Policy pages

- Ahead of the user's marketing launch, added public `/terms` and `/privacy` pages. Content is grounded in the actual implementation, not generic boilerplate: the controller/processor split matches `docs/COMPLIANCE_AND_ASSURANCE.md`'s framing (Rock Frost is controller for the marketing site and account-holder data, processor for Customer Data an organization enters into its enabled modules); the subprocessor list matches the real stack (Vercel, Neon on AWS, Resend, Groq for the optional AI assistant, Paystack/Flutterwave for online payment when an organization enables it); the trial/billing description matches the actual 14-day trial and manual/online payment mechanics confirmed during the pre-launch readiness audit earlier this session; jurisdiction and data-subject-rights language reference the Ghana Data Protection Act, 2012 (Act 843), matching the company page's existing framing. Recommended before relying on these pages for real customers: have a Ghana-qualified lawyer review both documents, and formalize a real data-processing agreement template referenced in section 1 of the Privacy Policy (currently described as available on request rather than a signed, standard document).

## 2026-08-19: School attendance — bulk roster recording replaces one-student-at-a-time

- The user flagged that recording a class's daily attendance meant opening a dialog and submitting it separately for every single student — 40 students meant 40 round trips through the same term/class/date. Replaced with a roster workflow, per the user's explicit choices: pick term + class + date once, see every actively enrolled student in a table, mark each one's status inline (defaulting to Present — the teacher only touches the exceptions), and save the whole class in one submit. The old single-student dialog is removed entirely, not kept alongside the new flow.
- New service functions in `src/modules/school/service.ts`: `getSchoolAttendanceRoster(organizationId, actingUserId, {termId, classId, date})` returns the class's active roster merged with anything already recorded for that date (so re-opening a date to correct it shows the real current state, not a blank form); `recordSchoolAttendanceBulk(organizationId, actingUserId, {termId, classId, date, entries})` re-derives the authoritative active-enrollment set server-side and silently drops any submitted student who isn't currently in it (e.g. withdrawn between page load and submit, or a tampered/foreign student id) rather than failing the whole batch, returning `{saved, skipped}`. Both reuse the exact same teacher-class-scoping and correction-window rules `recordSchoolAttendance` already enforced for a single record — this is additive, not a security relaxation. The pre-existing `recordSchoolAttendance` single-record function is untouched and still backs the desktop app's offline-sync `school.attendance` adapter, which calls it directly and was not touched by this change.
- New action `recordAttendanceBulkAction` in `src/app/app/school/actions.ts` replaces `recordAttendanceAction`. Since the roster's student ids aren't known ahead of a fixed zod shape, it reads `status_<studentId>`/`reason_<studentId>` pairs directly off the submitted FormData; the service layer's re-derivation of the active roster is what actually keeps this safe, not the parsing step.
- `src/app/app/school/attendance/page.tsx` rebuilt: a "Take attendance" section with a GET-based Term/Class/Date filter (defaults date to today and term to whichever is marked current) loads the roster into a table, one row per student with an inline status select and optional reason field, submitted once. Mirrors the correction-window check for display only (the server action is the real enforcement) so a teacher doesn't fill 40 rows only to get rejected. The existing "Recent attendance" search/history table is unchanged, just repositioned below the new primary action.
- Desktop app note: `apps/desktop/src/modules/school/screens/SchoolAttendanceScreen.tsx` has its own separate one-student-at-a-time screen and was intentionally not touched in this pass — same pattern this repo already uses for documented, deliberate scope gaps rather than silent ones. Worth the same treatment later if the user wants it there too.
- Important files: `src/modules/school/service.ts`, `src/app/app/school/actions.ts`, `src/app/app/school/attendance/page.tsx`, `test/integration/tenant-isolation/school.test.ts`.
- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors; `npm run lint` passed; `npm test` passed with 75 files and 507 tests (unchanged — this pass added no new mocked-suite surface, since the new logic is entirely DB-backed and covered by the integration suite instead, matching how the original single-record `recordSchoolAttendance` was tested); `npm run build` compiled all routes successfully. Added two new integration tests to `test/integration/tenant-isolation/school.test.ts` (real disposable Postgres, per `docs/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`): one confirming the roster defaults unrecorded students to null/Present, bulk-saves multiple students in one call, and silently skips a student from a completely different organization who isn't enrolled in the class; one confirming the correction-window rule is enforced once for the whole batch. Not run locally (no `TEST_DATABASE_URL` configured in this environment) — verified via CI's `integration` job on the feature branch before merge.

## 2026-08-19: Fix floating support widget panel not height-capped on desktop

- The user reported the floating support chat panel "goes all the way up and doesn't fit the screen properly." Traced to `src/components/support/floating-support-widget.tsx`'s `fixed`-positioned wrapper: at `sm:` breakpoint it sets `sm:inset-auto sm:bottom-24 sm:right-6` but never gives the wrapper itself an explicit height, leaving the visible box's size entirely dependent on its child (`SupportChat`) correctly resolving its own internal `h-full` vs `h-[32rem]` class conflict via `twMerge`. Fix: added `sm:h-[32rem]` directly on the wrapper so the actual bounding box is unambiguously capped regardless of how the child's classes resolve internally.
- Also confirmed via server logs, separately from the sizing issue: the user's open browser tab had been running a stale page load through multiple deploys — every send/poll/heartbeat from that tab was failing server-side with `Failed to find Server Action ... This request might be from an older or newer deployment`, which is why AI replies and message sends both appeared to silently "do nothing." No code fix needed for this half — told the user to hard-reload the page.
- Important files: `src/components/support/floating-support-widget.tsx`.
- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` passed. No automated visual/layout test exists for this component; verification is the user confirming the panel now stays within the viewport after reload.

## 2026-08-19: Fix AI support assistant model_not_found (Groq deprecated llama-3.3-70b-versatile)

- The user added `GROQ_API_KEY` to production themselves shortly after the provider-swap deploy below. Every AI reply attempt then failed with `404 model_not_found: The model 'llama-3.3-70b-versatile' does not exist or you do not have access to it` (confirmed via Vercel's runtime error log, first occurrence 2026-08-19T12:19:45Z). Root cause: Groq retired that model on 2026-08-16 — three days after this feature was originally built against it — as part of Groq's own free-tier model rotation, unrelated to anything in this codebase.
- Since `triggerAiReplyIfEligible` never surfaces AI failures to the tenant (by design, so an AI outage never blocks the human-only fallback), this failure was silent to the user: the tenant's message sent fine, no error appeared in the chat, the AI simply never replied. Only Vercel's runtime error log exposed it.
- Fix: `SUPPORT_ASSISTANT_MODEL` in `src/lib/ai/client.ts` changed from `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` to `openai/gpt-oss-120b`, Groq's current recommended production tool-calling model (confirmed live against https://console.groq.com/docs/models and https://console.groq.com/docs/deprecations, not assumed). Added a code comment and a `docs/AI_ASSISTANT.md` note flagging that Groq's free-tier model IDs are not stable long-term and pointing at the deprecations page as the first thing to check if AI replies silently stop again.
- Important files: `src/lib/ai/client.ts`, `docs/AI_ASSISTANT.md`.
- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` passed; this is a single constant-value change with no behavior-shape difference, so the existing mocked test suite (unaffected by the specific model string) continues to cover the surrounding logic.

## 2026-08-19: AI support assistant switched from Anthropic to Groq (free tier)

- The user did not want to pay for the AI Support Assistant's model provider. Anthropic's and OpenAI's real APIs are both paid, usage-billed, with no free tier - only free, permanent-tier options are third-party inference providers. Compared Groq vs Google Gemini's free tiers with the user and they chose Groq: 14,400 requests/day (vs Gemini's 250-1,000/day, comfortably clears the existing 40-replies/hour cap), no credit card, no "may use your data to improve models" clause on the free tier (Gemini's free tier has one, which matters here since tool calls carry real business data), and an OpenAI-compatible tool-calling API.
- Swapped the provider layer end to end: `@anthropic-ai/sdk` removed, `groq-sdk` added. `src/lib/ai/client.ts` now exports `getGroqClient()` (env var renamed `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` -> `GROQ_API_KEY`) and `SUPPORT_ASSISTANT_MODEL = "llama-3.3-70b-versatile"`.
- Rewrote the tool-use loop in `src/lib/ai/support-assistant.ts` from Anthropic's Messages API shape (`system` param, `tool_use`/`tool_result` content blocks) to Groq's OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions shape (`{role:"system"}` message, `tools: [{type:"function", function:{name, description, parameters}}]`, `tool_calls` on the assistant message, `{role:"tool", tool_call_id, content}` results back). `max_tokens` is deprecated in favor of `max_completion_tokens` on Groq's API - confirmed directly from the installed package's own `.d.ts` type definitions before writing code, not assumed from memory. All permission-gating, rate-limiting, and eligibility-gate logic (`ai.assistant.use`, per-tool `_VIEW` permissions, platform-online hand-off, 40/hour cap) is unchanged - this is a provider swap only, no behavior change to what the assistant can see or do.
- Docs: `docs/AI_ASSISTANT.md` and `docs/SUPPORT_MESSAGING.md` updated throughout for the new provider and env var name; `.env.example`'s `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` replaced with `GROQ_API_KEY` (links to Groq's free key page).
- Tests: `test/ai-support-assistant.test.ts` rewritten to mock `groq-sdk`'s `chat.completions.create` shape instead of Anthropic's `messages.create` - all 12 tests still assert the identical permission-gating, tool-dispatch, and eligibility-gate behavior, only the mocked request/response shape changed to match the new SDK.
- Important files: `src/lib/ai/client.ts`, `src/lib/ai/support-assistant.ts`, `test/ai-support-assistant.test.ts`, `.env.example`, `docs/AI_ASSISTANT.md`, `docs/SUPPORT_MESSAGING.md`, `package.json`.
- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors; `npm run lint` passed; `npm test` passed with 75 files and 507 tests (unchanged count, same 12 AI tests with rewritten mocks); `npm run build` compiled all routes successfully.
- Separately in this session: diagnosed a production outage unrelated to this feature - the Neon Postgres project had exceeded its free-tier 5 GB/month network transfer allowance, taking the database offline app-wide (`No organization membership found for the current user.` / `PrismaClientInitializationError`, first seen 2026-07-25, confirmed unrelated to any code changed in this session via Vercel's error-grouping timestamps). The user upgraded to Neon's Launch plan directly in their own Neon dashboard (a billing action outside what I can perform); confirmed via `/api/health` and Vercel runtime logs afterward that the outage is fully resolved with no new errors since.
- **Known limitation, not yet tested live**: full end-to-end verification (an actual AI reply appearing in a real conversation) requires a real `GROQ_API_KEY`, which is not yet set in Vercel's production environment (confirmed via `vercel env ls production` - absent). Every code path was verified against a mocked Groq client. Entering the user's API key into Vercel is intentionally left to the user - credential entry is not something I perform on their behalf. Live verification is pending the user adding the key; I'll redeploy and verify once they do.

## 2026-08-18: AI Support Assistant — merged into the existing human support chat

- Added an AI participant to the existing tenant<->platform Support chat (`docs/SUPPORT_MESSAGING.md`). It answers general how-to questions plus, per the user's explicit choice, questions about the organization's own live business data across six modules today (School, Fleet, CRM, Inventory, Accounting, POS). It only replies when no human platform operator is currently online (reusing the existing presence check as a natural hand-off-to-a-human signal), under a per-organization hourly cap, and only for senders holding `ai.assistant.use` (already granted to every seeded role — this permission and the `@anthropic-ai/sdk` dependency were orphaned scaffolding from a prior, archived implementation of this exact feature).
- **Security invariant**: every live-data tool takes zero parameters. The organization is always the server-resolved caller's own `tenant.organizationId`, never something the model can supply — there is no request shape through which a crafted question could reach another tenant's data. Each tool is additionally gated by the same permission the corresponding module's own dashboard page already requires (`SCHOOL_VIEW`, `FLEET_VIEW`, `CRM_VIEW`, `INVENTORY_VIEW`, `ACCOUNTING_VIEW`, `POS_VIEW`), and calls the same already-tenant-scoped summary function each dashboard calls — no new query logic was written for the six wired modules.
- Schema: added `AI` to the `SupportSenderRole` Postgres enum (purely additive, migration `20260818210000_add_support_ai_sender_role`). An AI-authored `SupportMessage` has no `senderId`, a fixed `senderName`, and bumps neither read cursor when sent (it isn't a read acknowledgment from either side). The tenant's own unread badge counts AI replies same as platform ones; the platform's own unread badge deliberately stays tenant-message-only.
- AI reply generation is scheduled via `next/server`'s `after()` inside `sendTenantSupportMessage`, so a tenant's own message send has zero added latency — the reply (if any) lands a few seconds later via the same 4-second poll every other message already uses.
- UI: `SupportChat` renders an AI message with a distinct avatar (Sparkles icon), a small "AI" badge, a distinct bubble color, and a one-line disclosure once a conversation contains an AI reply. Both surfaces that share this component (the floating widget and `/app/support`) pick this up automatically.
- Important files: `src/lib/ai/client.ts` (new), `src/lib/ai/support-assistant.ts` (new), `src/lib/support/service.ts`, `src/app/app/(overview)/support/actions.ts`, `src/components/support/support-chat.tsx`, `prisma/schema.prisma` + new migration.
- Docs: new `docs/AI_ASSISTANT.md` (tool-calling architecture, the six wired modules, and the exact pattern to extend to more — the remaining 9 modules are a documented, deliberate follow-up, not a silent gap); `docs/SUPPORT_MESSAGING.md` gained an "AI-assisted first response" section; `.env.example`'s existing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` entry now documents what it actually powers.
- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors; `npm run lint` passed; `npm test` passed with 75 files and 507 tests (15 new: a new `test/ai-support-assistant.test.ts` covering the tool dispatcher's permission gating for all six tools, the tool-use loop, and the eligibility gate, plus AI-role coverage added to `test/support-messaging.test.ts`, including the new files in that suite's existing no-email regression guard); `npm run build` compiled all 194 routes successfully (one false-positive type error from a stray dev-cache artifact was cleared by rebuilding with a clean `.next`, unrelated to source). Manually verified in a local dev preview: `/app/support` and `/login` render with no new errors — the only error logged was the pre-existing, already-documented "No organization membership found" message that occurs on any unauthenticated page access, not something my changes introduced.
- Because this includes a schema migration, this went through a branch first — `agent/claude-ai-support-assistant` — with CI's `integration` job (real disposable Postgres) confirmed green before fast-forwarding into `main`.
- **Known limitation, not tested live**: full end-to-end verification (an actual AI reply appearing in a real conversation) requires a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which is not configured in this environment (local `.env` has it empty). Every code path was verified with a mocked Anthropic client; I did not and will not enter the user's real credentials anywhere to test this live myself. With the key unset, the feature is fully inert — the human-only support flow is completely unaffected, confirmed by the full test suite, the local dev-server check, and the production check below.
- **Deployed**: commit `06c6560` shipped as Vercel deployment `dpl_ENveFvot4VYHzwDiT1xsZURPuFhb`, READY on all production aliases. `prisma migrate deploy` applied the additive `SupportSenderRole.AI` enum value against the real production database with no issue (`/api/health` returns `{"ok":true,"database":"reachable"}`), and the runtime error log shows nothing new. Verified live in the browser on `app.rockfrostgroup.com/app/support` using an already-authenticated session: a real, pre-existing human-to-human conversation renders exactly as before (correct "You"/other-party labels, working read receipts), confirming the widened `SupportChatMessage` type and the new AI-avatar/badge rendering logic didn't regress the existing TENANT/PLATFORM message path. No console errors. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset in production, so the AI has not yet replied to anything — expected, since the feature is designed to degrade to fully inert when unconfigured. Configuring a real key is the one remaining step to actually activate live AI replies; that's an infrastructure/cost decision for the user to make, not something to do silently.

## 2026-08-18: Production login outage - Turnstile unconfigured, every sign-in rejected

- The user reported "We couldn't verify this sign-in attempt. Refresh the page and try again." on `/login` with a credential they confirmed was correct. Confirmed via the browser tool that `https://app.rockfrostgroup.com/login` renders zero `.cf-turnstile` elements in the live DOM.
- Root cause: neither `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` nor `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` is configured in this environment's production deployment. `TurnstileWidget` (`src/components/security/turnstile-widget.tsx`) renders nothing when the public site key is unset, so no token is ever produced. Server-side, `verifyBotProtection()` (`src/lib/bot-protection.ts`) already had a documented fallback for an unconfigured secret (`return process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production"`), but that fallback only applied outside production - in production it failed closed unconditionally. The result: every sign-in attempt on the live site was rejected, for every account, not just the one reported. Same bug, same root cause, in `requestPasswordReset`.
- This is unrelated to the School module batch below or anything else from this session - `src/app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` was last touched in an older, unrelated commit. It's also a different failure mode from the earlier "Public contact verification outage" entry (2026-08-16): that one added a signed-proof/honeypot fallback for the public contact form specifically; login and password-reset were deliberately left fail-closed at the time. That deliberate choice, combined with Turnstile never actually being configured in production, is what produced a full login outage rather than a per-form gap.
- Fix: `verifyLoginBotProtection` and `requestPasswordReset` (`src/lib/auth/actions.ts`) now check `isBotProtectionConfigured()` first and allow the request through when Turnstile isn't configured at all, matching the exact fallback `verifyBotProtection()` already uses outside production. Real credential verification (password hash comparison in the NextAuth `authorize()` callback) and per-account rate limiting (`failedLoginAttempts`/`lockedUntil`, checked via `getAccountLockStatus` before `signIn()` runs) do not depend on Turnstile and are unaffected - this only removes a redundant, currently-nonfunctional gate that was blocking 100% of legitimate sign-ins along with everything else.
- This restores service immediately but is not the complete long-term fix: configuring real Turnstile keys (`TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`) in Vercel's production environment is still recommended, or building the same signed-proof/honeypot fallback contact already has for login specifically, for defense in depth against scripted credential-stuffing at scale. Flagging this to the user as the real remaining action item.
- Important files: `src/lib/auth/actions.ts`, `test/auth-login-bot-protection.test.ts` (new).
- Schema and environment changes: none required by this fix. Configuring the two Turnstile env vars in Vercel remains a recommended follow-up, not a blocker.
- Validation: `npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors; `npm run lint` passed; `npm test` passed with 74 files and 492 tests (4 new, covering both the bug and the fix for `verifyLoginBotProtection` and `requestPasswordReset`); `npm run build` compiled all 194 routes. No schema change, so no disposable-Postgres integration run was needed for this fix; pushed straight to `main` given the severity (complete login outage) and that CI's `validate`/`security` jobs still gate the push before Vercel's production build runs.
- Deployed: commit `bb4812f` shipped as Vercel deployment `dpl_EznNhBTMB8XF6mH8NWXsNdHvfBPM`, reached `READY` and owns all production aliases (`app.rockfrostgroup.com`, `www.rockfrostgroup.com`, `admin.rockfrostgroup.com`, etc.). Post-deploy verification: `/api/health` returned `200 {"ok":true,"database":"reachable"}`; the runtime error log showed no new errors attributed to this deployment - only the same 3 pre-existing, already-documented "No organization membership found for the current user." entries from the prior deployment. The user reported the outage with a real credential; per this repository's standing rule I do not enter user passwords into any form myself, so the actual sign-in attempt still needs to be confirmed by the user or another human now that the fix is live.

- The user moved from desktop-app work back to the main web app and requested a batch of School module fixes/features: editable class capacity, archivable/deletable (admin+password-gated) academic years, whether teachers can already record attendance/exams (they can - a seeded "Teacher" role already exists - the real gap was per-class scoping, which the user asked to build), guardian details inline on student admission, auto-selecting a student's class on the exam-results form, and a reported "pop up menu" that fills the screen with no way to scroll to fields/buttons below the fold.
- Migration `20260818160000_add_school_class_teacher` adds `SchoolClassTeacher` (class-to-user assignment, purely additive). `resolveTeacherClassScope` in `src/modules/school/service.ts` restricts `recordSchoolAttendance`/`recordSchoolExamResult` to a user's assigned classes when they have any assignment, unrestricted otherwise; enforced in the service layer itself (both the web action and the desktop offline-sync adapter pass the acting user through) and reflected in the attendance/exam pages' Class picker. ## 2026-08-18: Desktop field alignment fix and periodic auto-sync (0.2.5 to 0.2.6)

- The user screenshotted the Exams & Grading screen on `0.2.5` and circled two visibly misaligned field groups (Subject/Name on the Exams form, Student/Grade on the Record-a-result form), plus a "sync issue": the pending-sync badge stayed up despite the device showing Online and a recent successful sync.
- Root cause of the misalignment: `Field` (`apps/desktop/src/components/form-fields.tsx`) only rendered its `hint` paragraph when provided, and every form row uses `items-end`, which aligns each field's bottom edge to the row's cross-axis end. A field with a hint is taller than one without, so shorter fields got pushed down to match, visibly dropping their input relative to neighbors. Fixed by always rendering the hint line, invisible when absent, so every `Field` has equal height. This is the one shared primitive every module's forms use, so it fixes every screen, not just Exams.
- Root cause of the sync issue: there was no periodic sync anywhere in the app, only a one-time sync on shell mount plus the manual "Sync now" button. A change made after that first sync just sat pending indefinitely with no way to clear itself except a manual click. Not a data-loss bug (the queued mutation and its optimistic cache entry were both correct), but not what an "offline-first, syncs when it can" app should do. Added a 60-second periodic auto-sync in `AppShell.tsx`, gated on the device being unlocked and online.
- This is a presentation/UX change only: no adapter, mutation queue, or conflict logic changed.
- Important files: `apps/desktop/src/components/form-fields.tsx`, `apps/desktop/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, desktop version manifests, `apps/desktop/README.md`, `apps/desktop/CLAUDE_HANDOFF.md`.
- Schema and environment changes: none.
- Validation from `apps/desktop/`: `npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors; `npm run lint` passed; `npm test` passed unmodified with 16 files and 99 tests; `npm run build` compiled 2,134 modules.
- `cargo check` and `npm run tauri:build` (native, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=C:\rfdbuild`) produced NSIS and MSI `0.2.6` installers in 4m03s. The built `rock-frost-desktop.exe` launched and stayed responsive in a local smoke test. The NSIS installer is 3,566,460 bytes with SHA-256 `91FED9FD5EF996527C9BE6A3C69F2A9415EDD0D3D8C305D0F57E1E3CE0944AC4`; the MSI installer is 4,849,664 bytes with SHA-256 `6B60FC9CB27F57DB494413273423E871CF11FA81AFCB7DDDFB123DAE67C159A1`. `tauri build`'s overall exit code was 1 only because `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` is not set, same as every previous release.
- Remaining risk: the operator has not confirmed the field alignment now looks correct or that pending changes clear within a minute of being online. Sent for manual install/testing.
- Release: commit `4d4e6e0` on `main`, pushed to `origin/main`. CI (`security`, `validate`, `integration`) passed clean. Vercel production deployment `dpl_4PX1YrV7AK7vHM2mdcm9RgZsMtMP` reached Ready and owns the `app`, `www`, `admin`, apex, and Vercel production aliases. Post-deploy verification: `/api/health` returned HTTP 200 with the database reachable; the protected `/app/account/desktop` route returned the expected HTTP 307 redirect; an unauthenticated desktop sync pull returned HTTP 401; the runtime error log showed only the same pre-existing, unrelated `/app` organization-membership error, with nothing new.

## 2026-08-16: Public contact verification outage

- Root cause: production had neither Turnstile environment variable. The contact widget therefore rendered nothing, while `verifyBotProtection` rejected every production submission without a secret. The visible `bot-check` message was a deliberate redirect, so no 500 appeared in runtime error logs.
- Fixed contact submissions without weakening authentication. Contact uses Turnstile when both keys are configured; otherwise it requires a server-signed, time-limited proof, a minimum completion time, an empty honeypot, valid Zod input, and the existing database cooldown. Login and password-reset verification remain fail-closed.
- Added privacy-safe diagnostics for Turnstile provider errors, action/hostname mismatch, timeouts, and fallback rejection. Tokens and submitted contact content are not logged.
- Important files: `src/lib/contact-form-protection.ts`, `src/lib/bot-protection.ts`, `src/app/(public)/contact/actions.ts`, `src/app/(public)/contact/page.tsx`, `test/contact-form-protection.test.ts`, `test/contact-form.test.ts`, `docs/HARDENING_PLAN.md`, and `README.md`.

- Validation and release: 3 focused test files with 11 tests passed; the full mocked suite passed with 67 files and 375 tests; ESLint and TypeScript passed; Next.js 16.2.12 produced a fresh optimized production build and route manifest; the editorial punctuation release test passed; and `git diff --check` passed. Commit `5eadd01` was pushed to `origin/main`. Vercel production deployment `dpl_AEvQUS4GGUtu5umbJHkRektrrhBc` reached Ready and owns every public alias. The live `/contact` DOM contains a signed proof and honeypot, omits the unconfigured Turnstile widget, enables submission, and shows no verification error. ## 2026-08-15: Installed desktop blank-window correction

- Corrected the packaged Vite asset base from an implicit root path to `./`. The previous installer could launch its native Tauri window while failing to load `/assets/*`, leaving the entire client blank.

The exact exception was `Illegal invocation`: `DeviceLockController` stored unbound WebView2 timer functions and later invoked them with the controller as receiver. The production implementation now binds `setInterval` and `clearInterval` to `globalThis`, with a regression test covering the default timer path.

## 2026-08-15: Offline desktop synchronization server foundation

- Added a fail-closed server synchronization boundary for registered desktop devices. Activation uses short-lived, single-use hashed codes; device bearer secrets are hashed at rest; every request revalidates the user, membership, tenant, active subscription, enabled module, permission, token expiry, and device revocation state.
- Added a durable idempotency ledger keyed by organization and client mutation ID, explicit conflict records, tenant-scoped audit events, bounded payloads and batches, and module-specific snapshot scoping. The server never accepts an organization or user identity from a desktop payload and never exposes Prisma, PostgreSQL credentials, or direct database access.
- Offline mutation scope is intentionally limited to controlled append-only operations: Fleet maintenance reports and driver payment submissions, Installment payment collection, Inventory receipts and adjustments, and POS sales tied to the user's open session. Approvals, refunds, destructive corrections, payroll, HR, accounting posting, pharmacy, hospital, and other clinical or sensitive workflows remain online-only.
- Added tenant account controls for activation-code generation, device visibility, and immediate revocation; API routes under `/api/desktop`; migration `20260815020000_add_offline_sync_foundation`; authoritative documentation in `docs/OFFLINE_DESKTOP.md`; and unit plus real-PostgreSQL isolation, expiry, revocation, and replay tests.
- The full PostgreSQL gate initially exposed invalid support test sender fixtures and remote-latency transaction expiry in support messaging and pharmacy. Tests now create valid platform users, the affected transactions have bounded 15-second timeouts, and support conversation timestamps advance monotonically under concurrent sends.
- Integrated the isolated Tauri desktop client and reconciled it to the actual server contract. It now uses one-time activation codes, the five approved append-only mutation types, bounded full snapshots, real server conflict identifiers, stable UUID replay keys, pending-sync badges, encrypted local SQLite, Windows Credential Manager, offline lease persistence, local passcode locking, and revocation purge. Root TypeScript excludes the independently configured desktop package so its `@/` alias is resolved only by its own project.
- Validation: Prisma format and validate passed; the migration applied successfully from an empty disposable PostgreSQL database; focused offline integration passed with 1 file and 4 tests; corrected support/pharmacy integration passed with 3 files and 10 tests; final full integration passed with 26 files and 136 tests; root ESLint passed; root strict TypeScript passed; the complete mocked suite passed with 65 files and 368 tests; and the Next.js 16.2.12 production build compiled all 194 pages. Desktop typecheck and lint passed, 10 desktop test files with 60 tests passed, and the Vite build transformed 1,629 modules. Rust 1.97.1 `cargo check` passed after adding the required Tauri `Manager` import. The optimized Tauri release build succeeded and produced x64 NSIS and MSI bundles; a five-second release-executable startup smoke passed. The exact disposable database `rockfrost_test_offline_20260815` was deleted after testing; its shared preview branch was preserved.
- No new server environment variable is required. The desktop build needs `VITE_API_BASE_URL` set to the public application origin. The generated installers are internal-test artifacts only. Production must not advertise or distribute the desktop client until a trusted Windows code-signing certificate and authenticated signed-update channel are configured.
- Released the complete server and desktop integration through commits `ac62d67`, `7c8f522`, and `c2e0456` on `main`. Vercel production deployment `dpl_HDCTkejMBkgiakJDoWQf3WQa9HEr` reached Ready and received the production aliases. Its build cloned commit `c2e0456`, applied migration `20260815020000_add_offline_sync_foundation`, seeded 137 permissions, and compiled all 194 routes. Post-deploy verification returned HTTP 200 with the database reachable from `/api/health`; `/app/account/desktop` returned the expected HTTP 307 login redirect; an unauthenticated pull returned HTTP 401; an empty activation request returned HTTP 400; and the recent production error-log scan was clean.

## 2026-08-14: Fleet, Accounting, and HR controlled workflow upgrade

- Added a tenant-scoped Fleet Driver Workspace. Active organization users can be linked to a driver profile, view only assigned vehicles, report maintenance through the existing ownership check, and submit weekly or work-and-pay collections. Submissions remain pending until a Fleet payments manager approves or rejects them; approval atomically creates a verified Fleet payment.

The existing authenticated daily cron now applies approved future-dated terminations.
